\displaystyle\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}=\tilde{P}^{-1}\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{P}(\tilde{N}^{\prime}\text{div }_{{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}}\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\tilde{L}_{i}^{\prime}\partial_{i}\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}+\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}H)+\mathcal{R},

(3.85)

where
\|\mathcal{R}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0})
.
Now, we derive the estimate independent on
\epsilon^{\prime}
. Invoking (
3.85
), we have

\displaystyle\|\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0})+C(N_{0},M_{0})T\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}.

(3.86)

Since we always have the identity

\displaystyle\text{curl }\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}=\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}-\int_{0}^{t}\epsilon^{.ij}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{l}\partial_{l}w_{\varepsilon}^{j},

(3.87)

we can further write

\displaystyle\|\text{curl }\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0})+C(N_{0},M_{0})T\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}.

(3.88)

On the other hand, in light of (
3.84
), (
3.61
) and time evolving equation, we have

\displaystyle\|\text{div }\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0})+C(N_{0},M_{0})T\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}.

(3.89)

Notice that
\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}\bar{n}^{\kappa}=\bar{n}^{\kappa}-N
, the commutator estimate and(
3.61
) give us

\displaystyle\|\bar{\partial}\Lambda_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\cdot N\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma_{1}))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0})+C(N_{0},M_{0})T\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}.

(3.90)

Remark
.

In our case,
N=(0,0,1)
; the condition facilitates a more concise proof. In the general case, one can apply the trace inequality to control the commutator term, see
[
10
]
.

Combining (
2.27
), (
3.88
), (
3.89
), and (
3.90
), we have

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0})+C(N_{0},M_{0})T\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}.

(3.91)

Choosing small
T
if necessary, we achieve that

\displaystyle\|\Lambda_{\epsilon^{\prime}}*w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.92)

Letting
\epsilon^{\prime}\to 0
, we obtain
w_{\varepsilon}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))
and

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.93)

3.4
Proof of (
3.23
)

For
p\in H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
, we define a linear operator
Q(t):H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)\to H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
by Riesz representation theorem

\displaystyle\forall\varphi\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega),\quad\langle p,\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\varphi^{i}\rangle_{\frac{1}{2}}=(Q(t)p,\varphi)_{\frac{3}{2}}.

(3.94)

Clearly,

\displaystyle\|Q(t)p\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|p\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}.

(3.95)

Definition 3.2
.

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}(t)=\{v\in L^{2}(\Omega)|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}v^{i}(t)=0\}.

(3.96)

Due to
\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}(t)\cap H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)=R(Q(t))^{\perp}
, it implies that

\displaystyle H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)=R(Q(t))\oplus_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}(t)\cap H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega).

(3.97)

Definition 3.3
.

\displaystyle X(t)=\{\psi\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\psi^{i}\in H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)\}.

(3.98)

We define a linear functional
Q^{\prime}(t):H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)\to X(t)
via Riesz representation theorem, such that

\displaystyle\forall\varphi\in X(t),\quad\langle(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\varphi^{i},p\rangle_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}=(Q^{\prime}(t)p,\varphi)_{X(t)}.

(3.99)

One can find that
R(Q(t))
is closed in
X(t)
and

\displaystyle\|p\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|Q(t)p\|_{X(t)},

(3.100)

where the norm of
X(t)
is taken as a subspace of
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
. Owing to
\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}(t)\cap X(t)=R(Q(t)^{\prime})^{\perp}
, it yields that

\displaystyle X(t)=R(Q(t)^{\prime}))\oplus_{X(t)}\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}(t)\cap X(t).

(3.101)

Here, we will use two lemmas in
[
10
]
.

Lemma 3.5

(Lagrange Multiplier 1)
.

Let
\mathcal{L}(t)\in H^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
be such that
\mathcal{L}(t)\varphi=0
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{V}_{\bar{V}}(t)\cap H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
. Then there exists a unique
q(t)\in H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
, which is termed the pressure function, satisfying

\displaystyle\forall\varphi\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega),\quad\mathcal{L}(t)(\varphi)=\langle q(t),\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\varphi^{i}\rangle_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}.

(3.102)

Moreover, there is a
C>0
(which does not depend on
t\in[0,T]
and on the choice of
\bar{v}\in S_{0}
) such that

\displaystyle\|q(t)\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\mathcal{L}(t)\|_{H^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}.

(3.103)

Lemma 3.6

(Lagrange Multiplier 2)
.

Let
\mathcal{L}(t)\in X(t)^{\prime}
be such that
\mathcal{L}(t)\varphi=0
for any
\varphi\in\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}(t)\cap H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
. Then there exists a unique
q(t)\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
, which is termed the pressure function, satisfying

\displaystyle\forall\varphi\in X(t),\quad\mathcal{L}(t)(\varphi)=\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\varphi^{i},q(t)\rangle_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}.

(3.104)

Moreover, there is a
C>0
(which does not depend on
t\in[0,T]
and on the choice of
\bar{v}\in S_{0}
) such that

\displaystyle\|q(t)\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\lesssim\|\mathcal{L}(t)\|_{X(t)^{\prime}}.

(3.105)

Recall that (
3.61
)

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}w_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma_{1}))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.106)

Passing
\varepsilon\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} w_{\varepsilon}&\rightharpoonup w\quad\text{in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))$},\\
\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}(w_{\varepsilon})&\rightharpoonup\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}(w)\quad\text{in $L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))$},\\
w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}&\rightharpoonup w\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\text{in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Gamma_{1}))$}.\end{aligned}

(3.107)

The reason why
\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}(w)
and
w\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}
precisely represent the weak limit is akin to the explanation provided earlier. Further, by the definition of weak limit,

\displaystyle\|\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}w\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}=0.

(3.108)

We can employ a similar approach in step 2: 1)
\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}w=0
provides us with
L_{t}^{2}H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
regularity of the divergence, 2) confirm the mollified function in
L^{2}_{t}H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
, 3) find the uniform bound of the approximation in
L^{2}_{t}H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
via Grönwall-type inequality, 4) choose an appropriate small time and justify
w\in L^{2}_{t}H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
, which leads us to:

\displaystyle\|w\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.109)

For any
y\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
and
l=(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}y^{i}
, we can find a solution to the following elliptic equation:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}((\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}\varphi)&=l\quad\textit{in $\Omega$,}\\
\varphi&=0\quad\textit{on $\partial\Omega$.}\end{aligned}

(3.110)

Let
e^{i}=(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}\varphi
and
s=y-e
. We check that
s,e\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
and by orthogonal relation (
3.97
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}s^{i}=0,\quad\textit{$s(t)\in\mathcal{V}_{\bar{v}}\cap H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)$ a.e. t},\\
\|e\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}+\|s\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\lesssim\|y\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}.\end{aligned}

(3.111)

Since
(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}w^{i}=0
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}w_{t}^{i}&=-\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})^{j}_{i}\partial_{j}w^{i}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega))\\
\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{t},e\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}&=\int_{\Omega}w_{t}\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}\varphi=-\int_{\Omega}\partial_{k}w_{t}\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\varphi\\
&=(\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}w^{i},\bar{J}_{\kappa}\varphi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\end{aligned}

(3.112)

v
also satisfies equation

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{\varepsilon t},s\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}(w_{\varepsilon}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}=\sigma\int_{0}^{T}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}+\int_{0}^{T}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,s)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.113)

Now, we can check that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{\varepsilon t},y\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}&=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{\varepsilon t},e\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}+\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{\varepsilon t},s\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}(\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}w^{i},\bar{J}_{\kappa}\varphi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\sigma\int_{0}^{T}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}+\int_{0}^{T}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,s)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}(w\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&\leq C(N_{0},M_{0})\|y\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}.\end{aligned}

(3.114)

Hence, we deduce that for some small
\varepsilon_{0}
and
\varepsilon<\varepsilon_{0}

\displaystyle\|w_{\varepsilon t}\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))}\leq C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.115)

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
, we conclude that

\displaystyle w_{\varepsilon t}\rightharpoonup w_{t}\quad\text{in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))$}.

(3.116)

Likewise, we can confirm that
w_{t}
represents the weak limit on the right hand side of (
3.116
). Combining with
w\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
, we can conclude that
w\in C(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))
. Moreover,
w_{\varepsilon}(0)=u_{0}
gives us
w(0)=u_{0}
. Now, for all
\psi\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
such that
(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\psi^{i}=0
a.e.
t
, we define distribution
\mathcal{L}(t)\in H^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{L}(t)(\psi)&=\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{t},\psi\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}+\kappa(w\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\psi\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
-&\sigma(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\psi\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}-(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,\psi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\textit{a.e. $t$, $\forall\psi\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)$}.\end{aligned}

(3.117)

By Lagrange Multiplier 1, there exists
q\in H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \langle q(t),\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\psi^{i}\rangle_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}&=\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{t},\psi\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}+\kappa(w\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\psi\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&+\sigma(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\psi\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}+(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,\psi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\quad\textit{$\forall\psi\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)$, a.e. $t$}.\end{aligned}

(3.118)

Now, for all
\tilde{\psi}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
, integrating with respect to time, it yields that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}w_{t},\tilde{\psi}\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}-\int_{0}^{T}\langle q(t),\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\tilde{\psi}^{i}\rangle_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}+\int_{0}^{T}\kappa(w\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\tilde{\psi}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
=&\int_{0}^{T}\sigma(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\tilde{\psi}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}+\int_{0}^{T}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,\tilde{\psi})_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\end{aligned}

(3.119)

Hence,
w
is the weak solution. Suppose
\tilde{w}
is another weak solution in the space
L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega))
such that
\tilde{w}(0)=u_{0}
and
\tilde{w}_{t}\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)^{\prime})
. Taking
\bar{w}=w-\tilde{w}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}\bar{w}_{t},\tilde{\psi}\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}+\int_{0}^{T}\kappa(\bar{w}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},\tilde{\psi}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}=0.

(3.120)

Taking
\tilde{\psi}=\bar{w}
, after a simple calculation, we have
\bar{w}=0
. As a consequence, the weak solution
w
is unique. Later, we still denote the weak solution by
v
.

3.5
Proof of (
3.14
)

Now, we improve the regularity of
v
. The first step is to improve the regularity on the boundary. Then, we can utilize a similar strategy in the previous subsection to establish higher regularity. Similarly in
[
10
]
, we employ the difference quotient method. We use
D_{h}
to represent the difference quotient operator in the direction
e_{1}
or
e_{2}
. Invoking (
3.119
), setting
\tilde{\psi}=D_{-h}D_{h}v
, it yields that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}v_{t},D_{-h}D_{h}v\rangle_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}}_{(1)}\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{T}\langle q(t),\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}D_{-h}D_{h}v^{i}\rangle_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}}_{(2)}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{T}\kappa(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},D_{-h}D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}}_{(3)}\\
=&\underbrace{\int_{0}^{T}\sigma(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa},D_{-h}D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}}_{(4)}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{T}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,D_{-h}D_{h}v)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}}_{(5)}\end{aligned}

(3.121)

We analyze them term by term: by Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (1)&=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}D_{h}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}v_{t}),D_{h}v\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{J}_{\kappa}(x+h)D_{h}v_{t}D_{h}v+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v_{t}D_{h}\bar{J}_{\kappa}D_{h}v\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\|D_{h}v\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}-C(\|u_{0}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)})-C(N_{0})\|v\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{1}(\Omega)}-\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|_{H^{-\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}\|D_{h}\bar{J}_{\kappa}D_{h}v\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}\|D_{h}v\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}-C_{\delta}(N_{0})-\delta\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(3.122)

Notice that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}D_{-h}D_{h}v^{i}&=-D_{-h}D_{h}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}v^{i}-D_{h}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})D_{-h}\partial_{j}v^{i}(x+h)\\
&-D_{-h}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})D_{h}\partial_{j}v^{i}(x-h).\end{aligned}

(3.123)

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |(2)|&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}}C(N_{0})(\|D_{h}\partial v\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}+\|\partial v\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}})\\
&\lesssim C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0})+\delta\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}^{2}.\end{aligned}

(3.124)

The estimate of
(3)
reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (3)&=\sum_{i=1}^{2}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}_{i}(D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\bar{\partial}_{i}(D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}(D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})(D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\\
&+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}v(x+h)\cdot D_{h}(\bar{n}_{\kappa}\bar{n}_{\kappa})\cdot D_{h}v-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}_{i}(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\bar{\partial}_{i}(D_{h}v(x-h)\cdot D_{-h}\bar{n}_{\kappa})\\
&-\sum_{i=1}^{2}\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}_{i}(v(x-h)\cdot D_{h}\bar{n}_{\kappa})\bar{\partial}_{i}(D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(3.125)

Applying Young’s inequality again, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (3)&\geq\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}-C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0})-\delta\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}^{2}\\
&\geq\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}^{2}-C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0})-\delta\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\end{aligned}

(3.126)

(4)
and
(5)
can be controlled directly by

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |(4)|&\lesssim\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\|L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\|\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&\lesssim\delta\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}+C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0}),\end{aligned}

(3.127)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |(5)|&\lesssim\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|D_{h}v\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0})\end{aligned}

(3.128)

Remark
.

The estimate of
(1)-(4)
is identical to
[
10
]
. It can be verified that by introducing a higher-order term
D_{h_{1}}D_{h_{2}}...D_{h_{k}}
to equation
(5)
and evaluating its
L_{t}^{2}L^{2}{x}
norm, it can still controlled by
C(N_{0},M_{0},\kappa)
. To see this, because of the horizontal mollification of
\bar{J}^{\kappa}
,
\bar{A}^{\kappa}
, and
\bar{n}^{\kappa}
, their respective estimates in the
\bar{\partial}
direction remain controllable. The terms involving
\phi^{\kappa}
can be directly estimated using the properties of convolution.

Combining (
3.122
), (
3.124
), (
3.126
), (
3.127
), and (
3.128
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}^{2}\lesssim\delta\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}+C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.129)

The
\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}
and
\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}
estimates read

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}D_{h}v&=-D_{h}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}v^{i}(x+h)\\
\|\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}D_{h}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{0.5}(\Omega)}^{2}&\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}),\end{aligned}

(3.130)

and

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}v(t)&=\text{curl }u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}\partial_{l}v^{j}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{l}-\int_{0}^{t}\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}(\partial_{t}\phi^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}+\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}|\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}|^{2})\\
&=\text{curl }u_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}\partial_{l}v^{j}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{l}-\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{k}\phi^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}.\end{aligned}

(3.131)

Moreover,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}D_{h}v(t)&=-\varepsilon^{.ij}D_{h}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}v^{j}(x+h)+D_{h}\text{curl }u_{0}\\
&+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}D_{h}(\partial_{l}v^{j}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{l})-\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}D_{h}((\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{k}\phi^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa})\\
\|\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}D_{h}v(t)\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{0.5}}^{2}&\lesssim C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0})+\delta\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\end{aligned}

(3.132)

Hence, combining (
3.129
), (
3.130
), and (
3.132
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0})+\delta\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}

(3.133)

Choosing
\delta
small, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim C_{\delta}(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.134)

Consequently, the trace inequality shows that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|D_{h}v\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}^{2}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.135)

Passing
h\to 0
, we get the desired inequality

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|v\|_{H^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}^{2}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.136)

Combining this with the argument in previous subsection, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|v\|_{H^{2.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.137)

Now, we estimate the pressure with the Lagrangian multiplier 2. For any
y\in X
,
\varphi
is a solution of the elliptic problem

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}\varphi)&=\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}y^{i}\quad\textit{in $H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)$},\\
\varphi&=0\quad\textit{on $\partial\Omega$}.\end{aligned}

(3.138)

By interpolation theorem,
\varphi
is solvable and
\varphi\in H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)
. We still introduce
e^{i}=\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}\varphi
and
s=y-e
, we have
e\in H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
and
s\in\mathcal{V}\cap H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
. By orthogonal relationship, we have
\|e\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}+\|s\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\leq\|y\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}v_{t},e\rangle_{X}=(\partial_{t}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}v^{i},\varphi)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

(3.139)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{J}_{\kappa}v_{t},s\rangle_{X}+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}(1-\Delta)(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}=\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}+\int_{0}^{T}(\bar{J}_{\kappa}H,s)_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.

(3.140)

Notice that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}(1-\bar{\Delta})(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}&=-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\frac{(1-\bar{\Delta})(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})}{|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}|})s^{i}\\
&=-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{J}^{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\frac{1}{|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}|})(1-\bar{\Delta})(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})s^{i}\\
&-\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\frac{\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}}{|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}|}((1-\bar{\Delta})\partial_{j}(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}))s^{i}.\end{aligned}

(3.141)

Thus,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}(1-\bar{\Delta})(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})s\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}|&\lesssim\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\frac{1}{|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}|})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|(1-\bar{\Delta})(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|s^{i}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|((1-\bar{\Delta})\partial_{j}(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}))\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\|\frac{\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}}{|\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}|}s^{i}\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0})+\kappa\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{j}(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\|_{H^{\frac{3}{2}}(\Omega)}^{2}\\
&\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}).\end{aligned}

(3.142)

The right hand side of (
3.140
) can be directly controlled by
C(N_{0},M_{0})
. Hence, we can verify that
v_{t}\in L^{2}(0,T;X^{\prime})
with

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|_{X^{\prime}}^{2}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.143)

Invoking the Lagrangian multiplier 2, we can show that there exists a
q\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega))
with the estimate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|q\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}),

(3.144)

such that
(v,q)
is the weak solution to the approximating equation. Hence, by the uniqueness of the weak solution, we show that
\|q\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0})
. Now, we utilize the higher order difference quotient
D_{-h_{1}}D_{-h_{2}}D_{h_{2}}D_{h_{1}}v
to test the equation. Using a similar method, we can show
v\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{\frac{7}{2}}(\Omega))
. The improvement of regularity of
q
can be achieved by solving elliptic equation

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}((\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}q)&=-\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\bar{v}^{\kappa}:\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}v-\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\cdot(\phi^{\kappa}_{t}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa})-\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}|\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}|^{2}\quad\textit{in $\Omega$},\\
q&=-\sigma(\frac{\sqrt{\bar{h}}}{\sqrt{\bar{h}_{\kappa}}}\Delta_{\bar{h}}(\bar{\eta})\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})+\kappa\frac{1}{\sqrt{\bar{h}_{\kappa}}}((1-\bar{\Delta})(v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}))\quad\textit{on $\Gamma_{1}$},\\
(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{j}^{i}\partial_{i}q&=-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{\kappa}_{N}|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi^{\kappa}|^{2}\quad\textit{on $\Gamma_{0}$}.\end{aligned}

(3.145)

It’s clear that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|q\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(\|v\|_{H^{3.5}(\Omega)},\|\bar{v}^{\kappa}\|_{H^{4}(\Gamma_{1})},\|\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)})\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}),

(3.146)

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|v_{t}\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim C(N_{0},M_{0}).

(3.147)

Remark
.

Roughly speaking, the regularity of
q
is approximately two derivatives less than that of
v
. Consequently, terms involving
\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}
are consistently more regular than
q
. The curl estimate primarily encompasses terms like
\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi_{t}^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}
. Estimates in the horizontal direction can be regarded as smooth. Hence, the framework established by Coutand & Shkoller
[
10
]
is applicable in this context. The bootstrap argument stops when it encounters the regularity constraint
\Delta_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot n^{\kappa}
.

After this bootstrap argument, we can obtain

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{13.5}}+\|q\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{11.5}}+\|v_{t}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{10.5}}&\lesssim_{\kappa}C(N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}),\end{aligned}

(3.148)

in particular,

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|v\|_{12}\lesssim_{\kappa}C(N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}).

(3.149)

Now, we estimate the energy of the highest order term.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{curl }v&=\text{curl }u_{0}-\partial_{l}v^{j}\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}+\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}\partial_{l}v^{j}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{l}-\int_{0}^{t}\varepsilon^{.ij}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{t}\partial_{k}\phi^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}\\
\text{div }v&=-\partial_{j}v^{i}\int_{0}^{t}\partial_{t}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\end{aligned}

(3.150)

Thus, a similar estimate shows

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\text{curl }v\|_{12.5}&\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}+\|v\|_{13.5}\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\bar{A}^{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|v\|_{L^{\infty}}\int_{0}^{T}\|\partial_{t}\bar{A}^{\kappa}\|_{12.5}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{A}^{\kappa}\partial_{t}\partial\phi^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi^{\kappa}\|_{12.5}\\
&\lesssim\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}+\|v\|_{13.5}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}),\\
\|\text{curl }v\|_{11.5}&\lesssim\|v\|_{12.5}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{1},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5})+C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}),\\
\|\text{div }v\|_{12.5}&\lesssim\|v\|_{13.5}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}),\\
\|\text{div }v\|_{11.5}&\lesssim\|v\|_{12.5}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5})+C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).\end{aligned}

(3.151)

We estimate
\sup_{t}\|v\|_{12.5}
first. Taking
\bar{\partial}^{10}
on the velocity equation, testing
D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}\partial_{t}v
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{J}_{\kappa}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\langle D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}\rangle_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}^{10}q\bar{J}^{\kappa}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\cdot D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}\bar{J}^{\kappa}\bar{\partial}^{9}v_{t}D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\bar{\partial}^{9}(1-\Delta)v\cdot\bar{\partial}\bar{n}_{\kappa}D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\\
&+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}(\bar{J}^{\kappa}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}\bar{\partial}^{9}qD_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}+\mathcal{R}\\
&=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}+\mathcal{R},\end{aligned}

(3.152)

where
|\mathcal{R}|\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5})
is the lower order term.

Remark
.

We calculate the number of derivatives to control the commutators. For example,
2\times 10.5=21
horizontal derivatives on
\partial_{t}v
has been controlled in
L_{t}^{2}L^{2}
. The commutators has at most
22-1-21
derivatives on
\partial_{t}v
. Thus, the we control the commutator term related to
\|D^{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}\partial_{t}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}L^{2}}
. Similarly, we can control the rest of commutators. As a result we would like to improve half derivative to obtain
\|\partial_{t}v\|_{L^{2}H^{11}}
and
\|v\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{12}}
.

Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\\
=&\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}\partial_{t}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}+\mathcal{R},\end{aligned}

(3.153)

where
|\mathcal{R}|\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5})
is the lower order term. Notice that

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{11}(\Gamma_{1})}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\|_{H^{11}(\Gamma_{1})}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).

(3.154)

\displaystyle|\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}|\lesssim\|D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).

(3.155)

Consequently, we also have

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{10}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})D_{-h}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}|\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).

(3.156)

Combining (
3.152
) and (
3.156
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{J}_{\kappa}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\langle D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}D_{h}\bar{\partial}^{10}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}\rangle_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).

(3.157)

Letting
h\to 0
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\partial}^{11}v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\sup_{t}\|\bar{\partial}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}\|_{H^{11}(\Gamma_{1})}\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).

(3.158)

On the other hand,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{curl }v_{t}&=-\partial_{l}v^{j}\int_{0}^{T}\varepsilon^{.ij}(\partial_{t}\bar{A}_{i}^{l})+\text{curl }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}H,\\
\text{div }v_{t}&=-\partial_{i}v^{j}\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}(\bar{A})_{j}^{i}.\end{aligned}

(3.159)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{T}\|\text{curl }v_{t}\|_{H^{10}(\Omega)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\text{div }v_{t}\|_{H^{10}(\Omega)}^{2}&\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).\end{aligned}

(3.160)

Combining (
3.158
), (
3.160
) and normal trace inequality with elliptic estimate, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|_{H^{11}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}).

(3.161)

Combining (
3.151
), (
3.158
), and (
3.161
), we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|v\|_{H^{12.5}(\Omega)}+\int_{0}^{T}\|v_{t}\|_{H^{11}(\Omega)}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}),

(3.162)

by choosing
T
small enough.

Taking
\bar{\partial}^{12}
on the velocity equation, testing
\bar{\partial}^{12}v
, the
L^{2}
estimate suggests that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\|\bar{J}^{\kappa}\bar{\partial}^{12}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\langle\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa},\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}\rangle_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&=\|u_{0}\|_{12.5}+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}^{12}q\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\cdot\bar{\partial}^{12}v+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}\bar{J}^{\kappa}\bar{\partial}^{11}v_{t}\bar{\partial}^{12}v+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\bar{\partial}^{11}(1-\Delta)v\cdot\bar{\partial}\bar{n}^{\kappa}\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}\\
&+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\bar{\partial}^{11}q\bar{\partial}^{12}v+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{12}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}+\mathcal{R},\end{aligned}

(3.163)

|\mathcal{R}|\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5})
is the lower order term. The
H^{-\frac{1}{2}}
-
H^{\frac{1}{2}}
argument will lead us to

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}\bar{J}^{\kappa}\bar{\partial}^{11}v_{t}\bar{\partial}^{12}v|+|+\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\bar{\partial}^{11}(1-\Delta)v\cdot\bar{\partial}\bar{n}^{\kappa}\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}|+|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}\bar{\partial}^{11}q\bar{\partial}^{12}v|\\
\lesssim&\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\partial}\bar{J}^{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\bar{\partial}^{11}v_{t}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\bar{\partial}^{12}v\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\partial}^{11}(1-\Delta)v\cdot\bar{\partial}\bar{n}^{\kappa}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}\|\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}^{\kappa}\|_{L^{2}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
+&\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{\partial}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{j}\bar{\partial}^{11}q\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\|\bar{\partial}^{12}v\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)}\\
\lesssim&T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}).\end{aligned}

(3.164)

Similarly, using
\text{div }_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}v=0
and
H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
-
H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Omega)
argument, we have

\displaystyle|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Omega}\bar{\partial}^{12}q\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\cdot\bar{\partial}^{12}v|\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}).

(3.165)

The last term can be estimated in a similar fashion

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Gamma_{1}}\sigma\bar{\partial}^{12}(L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}|&\lesssim\sigma\int_{0}^{T}\|L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{11}(\Gamma_{1})}\|\bar{\partial}^{12}v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{1}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&\lesssim\sup_{t}\|L_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}\|_{H^{11}(\Gamma_{1})}T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5})\\
&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5})\end{aligned}

(3.166)

Hence, combining (
3.151
), (
3.163
), (
3.164
), (
3.165
) and (
3.166
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|v\|_{13.5}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}C(\kappa,N_{0},M_{0},\|u_{0}\|_{13.5}).

(3.167)

Now, choosing
N_{0}
large enough compared to
\|u_{0}\|_{18.5}
and
T
small enough such that
\|v\|_{L_{r}^{2}H^{13.5}}\leq N_{0}
. Thus, we define a functional
A:C_{T}\to C_{T}
. Similarly in
[
10
]
, it suffices to show
A
is sequentially weakly lower semi-continuous. To this end, suppose
(\bar{v}^{n})_{n=0}^{\infty}
is a sequence in
L^{2}(0,T;H^{15.5}(\Omega))
such that

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{n}\rightharpoonup\bar{v}\quad\textit{in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{13.5}(\Omega))$}.

(3.168)

It’s clear that
\bar{v}\in C_{T}
. By Rellich compactness theorem,

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{n}\to\bar{v}\quad\textit{in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{12.5}(\Omega))$}.

(3.169)

Now, since convolution operator, extension operator and Laplacian operator are linear, it’s clear that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\bar{v}^{n}_{\kappa}-\bar{v}_{\kappa}\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{12.5}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\|\rho_{\kappa}*E(\bar{v}^{n}-\bar{v})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{12.5}}+\|\Lambda_{\kappa}^{2}(\bar{v}^{n}-\bar{v})\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{12}(\partial\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\|\bar{v}^{n}-\bar{v}\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{12.5}(\Omega)}\\
&\to 0\quad\textit{when $n\to\infty$}.\end{aligned}

(3.170)

Hence,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \bar{v}^{n}_{\kappa}\to\bar{v}_{\kappa}\quad\textit{in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{12.5}(\Omega))$},\\
\bar{\eta}^{n}_{\kappa}\to\bar{\eta}_{\kappa}\quad\textit{in $L^{2}(0,T;H^{12.5}(\Omega))$}.\end{aligned}

(3.171)

The readers may refer to the method of Theorem 4.5 in
[
43
]
to show the convergence of
\phi^{n}
. Equivalently, we consider following system.
Let
(\bar{v}^{(1)},\phi^{(1)})
and
(\bar{v}^{(2)},\phi^{(2)})
be two pairs solutions to (
3.20
). Still denoting
\phi^{(1)}-\phi^{(2)}
by
\tilde{\Psi}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}\tilde{\Psi}-\partial_{i}(\bar{g}^{(1)}_{ij}\partial_{j}\tilde{\Psi})&=\partial_{i}((\bar{g}_{ij}^{(1)}-\bar{g}_{ij}^{(2)})\partial_{j}\phi^{(2)})+\tilde{\Psi}|\nabla_{\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(1)}}\phi^{(1)}|^{2}+\phi^{(2)}((\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(1)})_{i}^{j}-(\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(2)})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi^{1}((\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(1)})_{i}^{j}+(\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(2)})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi^{1}\\
&+\phi^{(2)}\nabla_{\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(2)}}\tilde{\Psi}\nabla_{\bar{A}_{\kappa}^{(2)}}(\phi^{(1)}-\phi^{(2)})\quad\textit{in $\Omega$},\\
(g_{\kappa}^{(1)})^{3i}\partial_{i}\tilde{\Psi}&=((g_{\kappa}^{(2)})^{3i}-(g_{\kappa}^{(1)})^{3i})\partial_{i}\phi^{(2)}\quad\textit{on $\partial\Omega$}.\end{aligned}

(3.172)

The standard parabolic estimate provides us the desired strong convergence. If we use
(\bar{v}^{n},\phi^{(n)})
,
(\bar{v},\phi)
to represent corresponding solution pair, we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \phi^{n}&\to\phi\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{2}H^{4}(\Omega)$},\\
\phi^{n}&\to\phi\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{\infty}H^{3}(\Omega)$},\\
\partial_{t}\phi^{n}&\to\partial_{t}\phi\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{2}H^{2}(\Omega)$},\\
\partial_{t}\phi^{n}&\to\partial_{t}\phi\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{\infty}H^{1}(\Omega)$}.\end{aligned}

(3.173)

Therefore, using the property of mollification, we have for some
s\geq 20

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \phi^{\kappa(n)}&\to\phi^{\kappa}\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{\infty}H^{s}(\Omega)$}\\
\partial_{t}\phi^{\kappa(n)}&\to\partial_{t}\phi^{\kappa}\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{\infty}H^{s}(\Omega)$}\end{aligned}

(3.174)

Now, we write
v^{n}=A(\bar{v}^{n})
. Then Eberlein–Smulian theorem implies that

\displaystyle v^{n}\rightharpoonup v\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{2}H^{13.5}(\Omega)$}.

(3.175)

Thus,

\displaystyle v^{n}\to v\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{2}H^{12.5}(\Omega)$}.

(3.176)

We want to show
v=A(\bar{v})
. To this end, first we can verify that

\displaystyle(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}v^{i}=0,

(3.177)

which is a result from strong convergence. Moreover, suppose
p^{(n)}
is solution to the following system:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}p^{(n)}&=-(\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}v^{(n)}_{k}(\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)})_{k}^{l}\partial_{l}\bar{v}^{(n)}_{i}\\
&-(\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\phi_{t}^{(n)}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)}}\phi^{(n)}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)}}\cdot\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)}}|\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa(n)}}\phi|^{2})\quad\textit{ in $\Omega$},\\
p^{(n)}&=-\sigma\Delta_{\bar{h}^{n}}\bar{\eta}^{(n)}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}^{(n)}-\kappa\bar{\Delta}_{0}(v^{(n)}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}^{(n)})\quad\textit{on $\Gamma_{1}$}.\end{aligned}

(3.178)

Similarly, applying Eberlein–Smulian theorem and compactness theorem again, it yields that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} p^{(n)}&\rightharpoonup p\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{2}H^{11.5}(\Omega)$}\\
p^{(n)}&\to p\quad\textit{in $L_{t}^{2}H^{10.5}(\Omega)$}.\end{aligned}

(3.179)

The strong convergence immediately yields that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} (\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{k}\partial_{k}p&=-(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}v_{k}(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{k}^{l}\partial_{l}\bar{v}_{i}-(\bar{A}^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}(\phi_{t}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\cdot\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}|\nabla_{\bar{A}^{\kappa}}\phi|^{2})\textit{ in $\Omega$},\\
p&=-\sigma\Delta_{\bar{h}}\bar{\eta}\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa}-\kappa\bar{\Delta}_{0}(v\cdot\bar{n}_{\kappa})\quad\textit{on $\Gamma_{1}$}.\end{aligned}

(3.180)

Now, with use of the strong convergence of
\partial_{t}v^{(n)}
in
L_{t}^{2}H^{12.5}(\Omega)
or by directly checking that
v
is the weak solution to the (
3.23
), we can deduce that
v=A(\bar{v})
. Hence, the Tychonoff fixed point theorem yields that there exists a solution to the
\kappa
-approximating problem.

Now, we consider the following steps: Given
\partial_{t}v\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{11.5}(\Omega))
, we can establish
\phi\in W_{2}^{3,6}(Q_{T})
. Then, we can obtain the existence and regularity of
\partial_{t}^{2}v\in L^{2}(0,T;H^{9.5}(\Omega))
. Finally, we can achieve that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}^{k}v&\in L_{t}^{2}H^{13.5-3k}(\Omega)\cap L_{t}^{\infty}H^{12.5-3k}(\Omega),\quad\textit{$k=0,1,2,3,4$},\\
\partial_{t}^{k}q&\in L_{t}^{2}H^{11.5-3k}(\Omega)\cap L_{t}^{\infty}H^{10.5-3k}(\Omega),\quad\textit{$k=0,1,2,3$},\\
\phi&\in W_{2}^{5,10}(\Omega\times[0,T]).\end{aligned}

(3.181)

4
Energy estimate I: Estimate for
\phi

We will employ a similar strategy in
[
10
]
. And a dedicated cancellation regarding to
\phi
will be applied to close the energy estimate.

Definition 4.1
.

\displaystyle E^{\kappa}(t)

\displaystyle=\sup_{t}[\sum_{s=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\eta\|_{H^{5.5-s}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|v_{tttt}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\sum_{s=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\phi\|_{H^{4.5-s}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}]

(4.1)

\displaystyle+\sum_{s=0}^{4}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\phi\|_{H^{5.5-s}(\Omega)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{6.5}^{2}+\sum_{s=0}^{4}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{s}v\|_{5.5-s}^{2}+1.

Since we only study the local well-posedness, we set
T_{\kappa}\leq 1
first.

Definition 4.2
.

We define

\displaystyle E_{\kappa}^{\prime}=\sup_{t}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\eta\|_{5.5-s}^{2}+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{6.5}^{2}+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{k}v\|_{5.5-k}^{2}

(4.2)

Remark
.

In the logic sequence, we first finish the estimate of
E_{\kappa}^{\prime}
and then
\|v_{tttt}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
,
\|v_{ttt}\|_{1.5}
. However, the last two terms are more difficult to analyze. So, in our writing sequence, we deal with these two terms first.

In the following section, we will denote
\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}(0),\|q(0)\|_{4.5},\|q_{t}(0)\|_{3.5},\|q_{tt}(0)\|_{2.5},\|d(0)\|_{9}
by
\mathcal{P}_{0}
. We want to show that they can be bounded by
\mathcal{P}(\|u(0)\|_{6},\|d(0)\|_{9},\sqrt{\kappa}\|u_{0}\|_{10.5})
. We consider the function satisfied by
q_{0}
.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Delta q_{0}&=-\partial_{j}v_{0}^{i}\partial_{i}(u_{0}^{\kappa})^{j}-\partial_{i}(\phi_{t}(0)\partial_{i}\phi(0))-\Delta(|\partial\phi(0)|^{2}),\quad\textit{in $\Omega$},\\
q(0)&=\kappa(1-\bar{\Delta})v^{3}(0),\quad\textit{on $\Gamma_{1}$},\\
\partial_{N}q_{0}&=0,\quad\textit{on $\Gamma_{0}$}.\end{aligned}

(4.3)

We can see that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|q\|_{4.5}&\lesssim\|\partial_{j}u_{0}^{i}\partial_{i}(u_{0}^{\kappa})^{j}\|_{2.5}+\|\partial_{i}(\phi_{t}(0)\partial_{i}\phi(0))\|_{2.5}+\|\Delta|\partial\phi(0)|^{2}\|_{2.5}+\kappa\|(1-\bar{\Delta})v^{3}(0)\|_{H^{4}(\Gamma_{1})}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(\|u(0)\|_{4.5},\|d(0)\|_{5.5})+\kappa\|v(0)\|_{6.5}.\end{aligned}

(4.4)

Similarly, we can derive estimates for
q_{t}
and
q_{tt}
. Readers may refer to
[
10
]
for further discussions on removing extra regularity. However, since the initial data for
\phi
is required to estimate
\partial_{t}^{4}\phi
, it cannot be removed due to its parabolic structure. This requirement
\|u_{0}\|_{6}
is higher than the velocity condition
\|u_{0}\|_{5.5}
in
[
10
]
.

We now provide a list of functions which represent the lower-order terms in our analysis.

Lemma 4.1
.

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{k}A^{\kappa}\|_{4.5-k}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),

(4.5)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}(\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{3.5-k}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)})\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.6)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\sum_{k=0}^{4}\|\partial_{t}^{k}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{4.5-k}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),

(4.7)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}A^{\kappa}\|_{3.5-k}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),

(4.8)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{3.5-k}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),

(4.9)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\sum_{k=0}^{2}(\|\partial_{t}^{k}\nabla^{\kappa}v\|_{2.5-k}+\|\partial_{t}^{k}\nabla^{\kappa}v^{\kappa}\|_{2.5-k})\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.10)

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\sum_{s=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\phi\|_{H^{4.5-s}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.11)

Proof
.

The estimate follows directly from the definition. We omit the details.

Similarly to the previous section, we further restrict
t
to be sufficiently small so that
g_{\kappa}^{ij}
remains close to
Id_{3\times 3}
in our analysis.

Lemma 4.2
.

Suppose there exists a constant
\tilde{N}_{0}
independent on
\kappa
such that
\sup_{t\in[0,T_{\kappa}]}E_{\kappa}\leq\tilde{N}_{0}
. Suppose
\varepsilon>0
is a small real number. Then there exists a time
T^{\prime}=\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{P}(N_{0})}
(independent on
T_{\kappa}
) such that
\forall t\in[0.T^{\prime}]
, the following estimate is true:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}\|(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}-\delta_{i}^{j}\|_{3.5}+\|(A)_{i}^{j}-\delta_{i}^{j}\|&\lesssim\varepsilon,\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}\|J_{\kappa}-1\|_{3.5}+\|J-1\|_{3.5}&\lesssim\varepsilon,\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}\|g_{\kappa}^{ij}-Id_{3\times 3}\|_{3.5}+\|g^{ij}-Id_{3\times 3}\|_{3.5}&\lesssim\varepsilon,\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T^{\prime}]}\|h_{\kappa}^{ij}-Id_{2\times 2}\|_{H^{3}(\partial\Omega)}+\|h^{ij}-Id_{2\times 2}\|_{H^{3}(\partial\Omega)}&\lesssim\varepsilon.\end{aligned}

(4.12)

Proof
.

We only take
A_{i}^{j}
as an example. Notice that the constant in the norm is value of function taken at
t=0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\|(A)_{i}^{j}-\delta_{i}^{j}\|_{3.5}\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}(A)_{i}^{j}\|_{3.5}\lesssim t\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.13)

If the time
t\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})}
, the lemma follows immediately. The other term can be proved in a similar way.

Remark
.

Note that if we have shown the existence of a
\kappa
-independent
t
such that
E\lesssim\tilde{N}_{0}
, this a priori assumption can be immediately recovered. (i.e., once this is established, repeating the procedure will yield the same estimate. Therefore, we do not explicitly specify
\sup_{t}
over a particular interval.)

4.1
Estimate for
\phi

In the following section, we denote
\phi|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi|^{2}
by
f
. Consider

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\phi-\partial_{i}(g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)=f.

(4.14)

f
is always a lower order term:

Lemma 4.3
.

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{4}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{k}f\|_{4.5-k}^{2}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.15)

Proof
.

Our focus is solely on the term that includes
\partial_{t}^{4}f
; the remaining terms are relatively easier to control.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{t}^{4}f&=\partial_{t}^{4}\phi|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi|^{2}+4\partial_{t}^{3}\phi(2\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\nabla^{\kappa}\phi)+6\partial_{t}^{2}\phi(2\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\nabla^{\kappa}\phi+2\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi)\\
&+4\partial_{t}\phi(2\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\nabla^{\kappa}\phi+6\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi)+\phi(2\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\nabla^{\kappa}\phi+8\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi+6\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi),\end{aligned}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}^{2}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})),\end{aligned}

(4.17)

Thus,

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{0.5}(\Omega)}\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.18)

We now begin our analysis with
\partial_{t}^{4}\phi
.

Proposition 4.4

(Estimate for
\partial_{t}^{4}\phi
)
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.19)

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{2}(\Omega)}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).

(4.20)

Proof
.

Taking
\partial_{t}^{4}
again on (
4.14
), testing
\partial_{t}^{4}\phi
, and integrating by part, it follows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi&=\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi(0)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}f\partial_{t}^{4}\phi,\end{aligned}

(4.21)

where we use the boundary condition
\nabla_{N}\phi=0
. Now, we can express the second term as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\end{aligned}

(4.22)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \partial_{j}\phi&=\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa})\partial_{j}\phi+4\partial_{t}^{3}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\\
&+6\partial_{t}^{2}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{j}\phi+4\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{j}\phi\end{aligned}

(4.23)

With the help of assumption
g_{\kappa}^{ij}\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta^{ij}
, invoking Young’s inequality, and choosing sufficiently small
\delta
, we can infer from (
4.22
) that

\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi|^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial\phi|^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\delta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial\phi|^{2}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+C(\delta)(T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).

(4.24)

After choosing small
\delta
, we can show that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;H^{1}(\Omega))}&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi(0)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{L^{2}(0,T;L^{2}(\Omega))}\end{aligned}

(4.25)

Similarly, a standard parabolic estimate gives us

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{2}(\Omega))}\lesssim(1+\|g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}(\Omega)})(\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi(0)\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}+\|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{1}(\Omega))}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))}).

(4.26)

We obtain the following estimate via interpolation between (
4.25
) and (
4.26
):

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{1.5}(\Omega))}\lesssim(1+\|g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}H^{2}(\Omega)})(\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi(0)\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}+\|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{0.5}(\Omega))}+\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{L^{2}(0,t;L^{2}(\Omega))})

(4.27)

Now, a direct computation shows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{0.5}&\leq\|\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa})\|_{0.5}\|\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+4\|\partial_{t}^{3}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{0.5}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}\\
&+6\|\partial_{t}^{2}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{1.5}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{1}+4\|\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{0.5}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),\\
\|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{1}&\leq\|\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa})\|_{1}\|\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+4\|\partial_{t}^{3}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}\\
&+6\|\partial_{t}^{2}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{1}+4\|\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{1}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)},\end{aligned}

(4.28)

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{0.5}(\Omega))}

\displaystyle\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),

(4.29)

Combining (
4.15
), (
4.27
) and (
4.29
), we can show that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{H^{1.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.30)

On the other hand, we perform the
\kappa
-estimate for
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}(\Omega)}
as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{2}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+\sqrt{\kappa}\|[\partial_{t}^{4},g_{\kappa}^{ij}]\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(0,t;H^{1}(\Omega))}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{2}(\Omega)}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).\end{aligned}

(4.31)

We now introduce the higher regularity of
\phi
,
\partial_{t}\phi
,
\partial_{t}^{2}\phi
, and
\partial_{t}^{3}\phi
.

Lemma 4.5

(Quasi-linear elliptic estimate for
L_{t}^{2}
)
.

Let
L_{A}
be a divergence-form elliptic operator corresponding to a Neumann-boundary operator
B_{A}
as

\displaystyle L_{A}=\partial_{i}(g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{j})

(4.32)

\displaystyle B_{A}=(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\partial_{j}

(4.33)

Then, we can consider the elliptic problem (
k=0,1,2,3
) as

\displaystyle L_{A}(\partial_{t}^{k}\phi)

\displaystyle=\partial_{t}^{k}(\partial_{t}\phi-f)+[L_{A},\partial_{t}^{k}]\phi

(4.34)

\displaystyle B_{A}(\partial_{t}^{k}\phi)

\displaystyle=-[\partial_{t}^{k},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi.

(4.35)

The commutator terms read

\displaystyle[L_{A},\partial_{t}]\phi

\displaystyle=-\partial_{i}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{j}\phi-\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi,

(4.36)

\displaystyle-[\partial_{t},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi

\displaystyle=-\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi

(4.37)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \phi&=-\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{2}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{j}\phi-2\partial_{i}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi-\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi-2\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\\
-[\partial_{t}^{2},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi&=-\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi-2\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi\end{aligned}

(4.38)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \phi&=-\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{j}\phi-\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi-3\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\\
&-3\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi-3\partial_{i}\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{j}\phi-3\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\\
\\
-[\partial_{t}^{3},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi&=-\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi-3\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi-3\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\phi\end{aligned}

(4.39)

The following elliptic estimate holds:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\phi\|_{5.5-k}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),\\
\sum_{k=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\phi\|_{5.5-k}^{2}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.40)

Proof
.

From a direct computation, using Sobolev trace lemma, we can show that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|[L_{A},\partial_{t}]\phi\|_{2.5}&\lesssim\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{2.5}\|\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2.5}+\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2.5}\|\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2.5}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),\\
\|-[\partial_{t},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi\|_{H^{3}(\partial\Omega)}&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{3.5}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})\end{aligned}

(4.41)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|[L_{A},\partial_{t}^{2}]\phi\|_{1.5}&=\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{2}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{1.5}\|\|\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\|_{1.5}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{1.5}\|\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{1.5}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),\\
\|-[\partial_{t}^{2},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\partial\Omega)}&\lesssim\|-\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi-2\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{2.5}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.42)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|[L_{A},\partial_{t}^{3}]\phi\|_{0.5}&\lesssim\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{0.5}\|\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{0.5}\|\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{0.5}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}\\
&\|\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{0.5}+\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{0.5}+\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{0.5}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\\
\|-[\partial_{t}^{3},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\partial\Omega)}&\lesssim\|-\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi-3\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi-3\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{1.5}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\|_{1.5}\|\partial_{i}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\|_{1.5}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{1.5}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.43)

We commence the estimate at
k=3
. Invoking the Lemma 2.1, it yields that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{H^{2.5}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})(\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{{H^{0.5}}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}(\phi|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi|^{2})\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)}+\|[\partial_{t}^{3},L_{A}]\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\Omega)})\\
&+\|-[\partial_{t}^{3},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\partial\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}.\end{aligned}

(4.44)

Owing to (
4.15
) and (
4.43
), we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{H^{2.5}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.45)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{H^{2.5}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.46)

Inductively, applying an analogous argument, we deduce that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{k=0}^{3}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\phi\|_{5.5-k}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}),\\
\sum_{k=0}^{3}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{k}\phi\|_{5.5-k}^{2}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

To close the energy estimate, we also need two estimates of
\phi
involving
\kappa
.

Lemma 4.6

(Estimate of
\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H^{2}}
using
E_{\kappa}^{\prime}
)
.

\displaystyle\sup_{t}\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E_{\kappa}^{\prime})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})

(4.47)

Proof
.

Similar to (
4.43
), we have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|[L_{A},\partial_{t}^{3}]\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{2}\\
&+\|\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{i}\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E_{\kappa}^{\prime})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\\
\|-[\partial_{t}^{3},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\partial\Omega)}&\lesssim\|-\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{i}\phi-3\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi-3\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{1}\\
&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}^{3}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\|_{1}\|\partial_{i}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}^{2}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\|_{1}\|\partial_{t}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{2}+\|\partial_{t}g_{\kappa}^{3i}\|_{2}\|\partial_{t}^{2}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{1}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E_{\kappa}^{\prime})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.48)

The norm involving
\phi
has been controlled, and the norm consist of
g_{\kappa}
can be bounded by
E_{\kappa}^{\prime}
.
Now, since

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}^{3}f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})\end{aligned}

(4.49)

, and
\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}
has been controlled, we can write

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E_{\kappa}^{\prime})(\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}(\phi|\nabla^{\kappa}\phi|^{2})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}+\|[\partial_{t}^{3},L_{A}]\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\|-[\partial_{t}^{3},g_{\kappa}^{3i}]\partial_{i}\phi\|_{H^{0.5}(\partial\Omega)}+\|\partial_{t}^{3}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}(E_{\kappa}^{\prime})+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.50)

Lemma 4.7

(
\kappa
estimate for
\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{t}H^{6.5}(\Omega)}
)
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\phi\|_{6.5}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.51)

Proof
.

We consider following elliptic system:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \Delta\phi&=\partial_{t}\phi+\partial_{i}((\delta^{ij}-g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{j}\phi)-f\quad\textit{in $\Omega$},\\
\partial_{3}\phi&=(\delta_{j}^{3}-(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi\quad\textit{on $\partial\Omega$}.\end{aligned}

(4.52)

The Sobolev inequality shows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\partial_{i}((\delta^{ij}-g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{j}\phi)\|_{4.5}&\lesssim\|\delta^{ij}-g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{5.5}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}+\|\delta^{ij}-g_{\kappa}^{ij}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial\phi\|_{5.5}\\
&\lesssim\|\eta\|_{6.5}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))+\delta\|\phi\|_{6.5}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.53)

Similarly, the trace inequality implies that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|(\delta_{j}^{3}-(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi\|_{H^{5}(\partial\Omega)}&\lesssim\|\delta_{j}^{3}-(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\|_{H^{5}(\partial\Omega)}\|\partial\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}\\
&+\|\delta_{j}^{3}-(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j}\|_{L^{\infty}(\partial\Omega)}\|\partial\phi\|_{H^{5}(\partial\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\|\eta\|_{6.5}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))+\delta\|\phi\|_{6.5}+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.54)

Therefore, the elliptic estimate shows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\phi\|_{6.5}&\lesssim\|\partial_{t}\phi\|_{4.5}+\|\partial_{i}((\delta^{ij}-g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{j}\phi)\|_{4.5}+\|f\|_{4.5}+\|(\delta_{j}^{3}-(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{3}(A^{\kappa})_{i}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi\|_{H^{5}(\partial\Omega)}\\
\|\sqrt{\kappa}\phi\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{6.5}}&\lesssim T^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\eta\|_{6.5}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))+\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})\\
&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).\end{aligned}

(4.55)

We provide the following corollary for
\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}
which will be used later.

Corollary 4.8
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

(4.56)

Proof
.

We compute it directly as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}&\lesssim\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}A^{\kappa}\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}\|\partial\phi\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}+\|A^{\kappa}\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}+\mathcal{R}\\
&\lesssim\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{H^{2}(\Omega)}(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))+\mathcal{R},\end{aligned}

(4.57)

where
\int_{0}^{T}|\mathcal{R}|^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})
is the lower order term. Integrating on time, we immediately obtain the desired estimate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})+(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))\|\sqrt{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}.

(4.58)

Next, we introduce the key lemma in our article.

Proposition 4.9

(key cancellation)
.

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\nabla^{\kappa}_{l}\partial_{t}^{4}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}\nabla^{\kappa}_{l}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\nabla^{\kappa}_{\mu}\phi=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi+\mathcal{R},

(4.59)

where
|\mathcal{R}|\lesssim\delta\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{1}^{2}+\delta\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+\delta\|\partial_{t}^{3}v\|_{1.5}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))
.

Proof
.

Taking
\partial_{t}^{4}
on (
4.14
), testing
J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{5}\phi
, and integrating by part in space, it implies that

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi|^{2}}_{K_{1}}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{2}}

\displaystyle=\mathcal{P}_{0}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}f\partial_{t}^{5}\phi}_{K_{3}}\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{i}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}(g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{5}\phi}_{K_{4}},

(4.60)

where we apply the Neumann boundary condition on
\phi
. After a direct calculation, the second term
K_{2}
on the LHS reads

\displaystyle K_{2}=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{21}}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}((A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22}}+\mathcal{R},

(4.61)

\mathcal{R}
can be written in the form of

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}(\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{5}(\partial_{i}\phi),\quad\textit{$s_{1}$, $s_{2}\geq 1$},\quad s_{1}+s_{2}=4.

(4.62)

Integrating by part in time,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \mathcal{R}&\sim\sum_{s_{1}+s_{2}=2}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{i}\phi)|_{0}^{t}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\phi))\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{i}\phi)\\
&-\sum_{s_{1}+s_{2}=2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{i}\phi),\end{aligned}

(4.63)

Recall that using our notation,
\partial\phi
,
g_{\kappa}
have at least order 4 (correspondingly, have been controlled in order 3). We directly calculate that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\mathcal{R}|&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\sum_{s_{1}+s_{2}=2}\|J_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\sum_{s_{1}+s_{2}=2}\int_{0}^{t}\|J_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\phi))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\sum_{s_{1}+s_{2}=2}\int_{0}^{t}\|\bar{\partial}J_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s_{1}}\partial_{t}(g_{\kappa}^{ij})\partial_{t}^{s_{2}}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\phi))\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}(\partial_{i}\phi)\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\delta\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{1}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).\end{aligned}

(4.64)

Similarly, we can write the major part in
K_{21}
as

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{21}&=\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{t}(\partial_{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi)\\
&=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}J_{\kappa}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}\partial_{t}(J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\partial_{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{21,1}}.\end{aligned}

(4.65)

One can compute that

\displaystyle|K_{21,1}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{2}\|\partial_{t}(J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}

(4.66)

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}).

(4.67)

Thus, combining (
4.61
), (
4.64
), (
4.65
), (
4.66
), we can express
K_{2}
as

\displaystyle K_{2}=\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}J_{\kappa}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}((A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22}}+\mathcal{R},

(4.68)

where
|\mathcal{R}|\lesssim\delta\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{1}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))
.
Now, we estimate
K_{3}
in (
4.60
), applying (
4.17
)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |K_{3}|&\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\|J_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}f\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\delta\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).\end{aligned}

(4.69)

The estimate for
K_{4}
is similar.
Using our order argument,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |K_{4}|&\lesssim\sum_{k=0}^{4}C_{4}^{k}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{i}J_{\kappa}\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{k}g^{ij}_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4-k}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\delta\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).\end{aligned}

(4.70)

Combining (
4.60
), (
4.68
), (
4.69
), and (
4.70
), we can summarize that

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}((A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22}}=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi|^{2}-\int_{\Omega}\frac{1}{2}J_{\kappa}g_{\kappa}^{ij}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi+\mathcal{R},

(4.71)

where
|\mathcal{R}|\lesssim\delta\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{1}^{2}+\delta\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}^{5}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa}))
.
Now, we calculate the major part of
K_{22}
.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{22}&=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{4}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22,1}}\underbrace{+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{4}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22,2}}\\
&\underbrace{+\sum_{s=1}^{3}C_{4}^{s}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{s}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{4-s}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22,3}}\end{aligned}

(4.72)

We estimate
K_{22,3}
first. Integrating by part in time,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{22,3}&=\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{4}^{s+1}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi|_{0}^{t}\\
&-\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{4}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(J_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\\
&-\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{4}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi.\end{aligned}

(4.73)

As a consequence,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |K_{22,3}|&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{4}^{s+1}\|J_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{4}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}(J_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&+\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{4}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\|J_{\kappa}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{i}\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{t}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\delta\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{H^{1}(\Omega)}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).\end{aligned}

(4.74)

From a direct calculation, we calculate the leading order term of
K_{22,1}
and
K_{22,2}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{22,1}&=\underbrace{-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k}\partial_{k}\partial_{t}^{3}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}(A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22,11}}\\
&\underbrace{-\sum_{s=1}^{3}C_{3}^{s}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}\partial_{t}^{s}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{3-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{5}\partial_{i}\phi}_{K_{22,12}}\end{aligned}

(4.75)

Integrating by part in time,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} K_{22,12}&=-\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{3}^{s+1}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi|_{0}^{t}\\
&+\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{3}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\partial_{t}(J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi)\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\\
&+\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{3}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu})\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi.\end{aligned}

(4.76)

Thus,

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |K_{22,12}|&\lesssim\mathcal{P}_{0}+\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{3}^{s+1}\|J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{3}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\|\partial_{t}(J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi)\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\sum_{s=0}^{2}C_{3}^{s+1}\int_{0}^{t}\|J_{\kappa}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{j}\partial_{j}\phi\|_{L^{\infty}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}(\partial_{t}^{s}\partial_{t}((A^{\kappa})_{\mu}^{i}(A^{\kappa})_{l}^{k})\partial_{t}^{2-s}\partial_{k}v_{\kappa}^{\mu})\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\|\partial_{t}^{4}\partial_{i}\phi\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}\\
&\lesssim\delta\|\partial_{t}^{4}\phi\|_{1}^{2}+C(\delta)(\mathcal{P}_{0}+T\mathcal{P}(E^{\kappa})).\end{aligned}

(4.77)

Similarly,
